### T06k — Windows from the library share per building type (`D-EU-140`, `FINDING 282`, owner 2026-10-01)

**Why.** `FINDING 282` (§8): no EU IDF of any vintage has a window; `build_idf_for_building` has no window step. Owner, 2026-10-01: "ok use the library window share per building type apply windows" (`D-EU-140 (a)`).

**Pinned rules (do not change).**
- Share per archetype: `share = ΣA_Window / (ΣA_Window + ΣA_Wall)`, from `openubem/data/construction/tabula_archetypes_<es|fr|gb|it>.json` `records[]` keyed by `archetype_id`.
  - `ΣA_Window = sum(geometry.a_window_components_m2)`.
  - `ΣA_Wall = sum(geometry.a_wall_components_m2)`.
- Fallback: when `ΣA_Window == 0` or `u_window_w_m2k` is not a number (today only `ES.ME.MFH.05.Gen.ReEx.001.001`, 2 Madrid homes), use the median share, `u_window_w_m2k` and `g_gl_window` over the same country's records with the same `building_type` that do have data. Set `fallback = True`.
- Glazing: one `WINDOWMATERIAL:SIMPLEGLAZINGSYSTEM` plus one `CONSTRUCTION` per IDF.
  - `U = (u_window_w_m2k + delta_u_tb_w_m2k) × f_red_temp`, the same reduction the walls get at `scripts/run_eu_s2_campaign.py:1809-1813`. Assert `U < 7.0`.
  - `SHGC = g_gl_window`.
  - `Visible_Transmittance = min(1, SHGC)`.
  - This is the S0 precedent at `openubem/idf/european_box.py:127-135, 290-293`. No frame fraction: TABULA's (1 − F_F) is not applied, the same as S0; record this in §8 Notes.
- Placement: every `BUILDINGSURFACE:DETAILED` with `Surface_Type = Wall` and `Outside_Boundary_Condition = Outdoors` gets one `FENESTRATIONSURFACE:DETAILED` of type `Window`.
  - The window is the wall polygon scaled about its centroid by `sqrt(share)`, with the same vertex order (3 or 4 vertices).
  - A wall is skipped (counted, with the reason) when its polygon is not convex, has more than 4 vertices, or when the window would be under 0.05 m².
  - No window on `Adiabatic`, `Surface` or `Ground` walls.
  - Names are `<wall name>_Win`. View factor `autocalculate`, no frame or divider, multiplier 1.
- Geometry is never touched. The output IDF equals the source IDF plus the window objects, the glazing material and the glazing construction.

**What / How.**
1. New `openubem/idf/european_windows.py`:
   - `add_tabula_windows(idf, record) -> dict`. The dict holds share_target, outdoor_wall_m2, window_m2, share_achieved, walls_with_window, walls_skipped, skip_reasons, u, shgc and fallback.
   - `window_record(archetype_id, country) -> dict`, which applies the share and fallback rules above.
   - Call `add_tabula_windows` at the end of `build_idf_for_building` (`scripts/run_eu_s2_campaign.py:1843`), after `_assign_envelope_constructions`, so every future build has windows.
2. New `scripts/eu21/10_add_windows.py --district <D> | --all`: applies `add_tabula_windows` to the exact IDF set that the 2026-10-01 harvest uses. Use at most 10 local processes and no EnergyPlus.
   - Universe: the stems of `<D>_recut_2026-09-08/prepared_buildings.csv` (4,171), minus the 58 in `division_residual_2026-09-29/undivided_excluded_2026-10-01.csv`.
   - Source IDF per stem, first match wins:
     1. listed in `<D>_fix_2026-10-01/shipped_t06g.lst` → `<D>_fix_2026-10-01_t06g/idfs/`;
     2. listed in `shipped_t06f.lst` → `<D>_fix_2026-10-01_t06f/idfs/`;
     3. one of the 5 held homes of `FINDING 280` (`d0787f31dc827e89`, `b56bda9013da5b2d`, `d342db5179c1ac49`, `d32f27af52ecab2c`, `a53d307eac631355`) → `<D>_recut_2026-09-08/idfs/`;
     4. present in `<D>_fix_2026-10-01/prepared_buildings.csv` → `<D>_fix_2026-10-01/idfs/`;
     5. otherwise (the 11 homes dropped at CP-2) → `<D>_recut_2026-09-08/idfs/`.
   - The schedules come from the same tree as the IDF (`schedules/<stem>/`). The `prepared_buildings.csv` row comes from the same tree.
   - Write `EU-11/<D>_win_2026-10-01/` containing:
     - `idfs/`;
     - `schedules/` (copied);
     - `weather/` (copied from `<D>_fix_2026-10-01/weather`);
     - `prepared_buildings.csv` (plus a `source_tree` column and a new `idf_sha256`);
     - `windows.csv`, one row per home with the dict fields, `archetype_id` and `source_tree`;
     - `fleet.lst`: every stem, longest first by the 09-08 `run_seconds` of `<D>_merged_2026-09-08/<slug>_manifest.csv`, else by floor area.
   - For the stems from tree 5, report `geometry_outcome` and the zone count; do not drop any.

**How to test.**
1. Unit tests in a new `tests/test_eu_windows_2026_10_01.py`:
   - rectangle wall with share 0.2 → window area 0.2 × wall within 1e-6, all vertices inside the wall, same normal;
   - triangle wall → the same holds;
   - `Adiabatic` and `Surface` walls get no window;
   - the fallback archetype gets fallback values and `fallback = True`;
   - U and SHGC match the formulas;
   - a non-convex wall is skipped with its reason.
2. Build ×4. Report per district:
   - homes written N of universe;
   - `fallback` count;
   - median and range of `share_achieved − share_target`;
   - homes with |difference| > 0.01, listed with the cause;
   - walls skipped by reason.
3. Geometry identity on 100 % of homes: remove the added objects from the output (window surfaces, glazing material, glazing construction). The result must equal the source IDF object for object (eppy, field by field). Any difference is a failure.
4. `ep_geometry_preflight` returns [] on every output IDF.
5. These test files are green: `tests/test_eu_windows_2026_10_01.py`, `tests/test_eu_floor_division_residual_2026_09_29.py`, `tests/test_eu_collinear_ring_cleanup.py`.
6. Write the §8 entry `#### T06k — windows — completed 2026-10-01` with the gate lines, then STOP.
   - The director runs a 40-home EnergyPlus sample on Speed first (largest share, triangles, fallback, every district), then the full arrays.
   - Register any error in the debug references.

### T06l — Harvest code for the windowed fleet `win_2026-10-01` (director 2026-10-01, after the T06k audit)

**Why.** Owner: "submit simualtiosn with windows, we can keep no window simulations as refernece". The windowed fleet is the set we publish. It covers the whole T06k universe: 4,113 homes, every IDF in `EU-11/<D>_win_2026-10-01/idfs/`, no t06f or t06g swap. A windowed home that fails has **no** result. Carrying a no-window or 09-08 EUI into a windowed total would mix two different models. The no-window `_merged_2026-10-01` set (T06d2/T06d3) stays as the reference only.

**What.** Edit only `scripts/cluster/harvest_eu11_district.py`, `scripts/cluster/harvest_eu11_merged.py` and `scripts/cluster/compare_eu11_eui_ratio.py`.
1. `harvest_eu11_district.py`, `harvest_district`: when `tag` starts with `win_`, the job list comes from `<dist_dir>/speed_jobs.txt` (one int per line, written by the director, in submission order) instead of `TAGGED_DISTRICT_JOBS`. If the file is missing, raise. Everything else is unchanged. No shipped lists exist in the win folders, so `idf_sha256` = prepared.
2. `harvest_eu11_merged.py`:
   - `WIN_TAG = "win_2026-10-01"`.
   - `merged_tag == "win_2026-10-01"` → new `_build_win_manifest(district, dry_run)`.
   - Base rows = `<D>_merged_2026-09-08/<slug>_manifest.csv`, which keeps all 4,171 building ids.
   - Win ids = the building ids of the win `fleet.lst` stems, mapped through `<D>_win_2026-10-01/prepared_buildings.csv`.
   - Not dry: `harvest_district(district, work_base, tag=WIN_TAG)`.
   - Per base row, first match wins:
     1. Undivided (`_undivided_ids`) → `excluded.csv` as today, no row.
     2. In win rc-0 → `eui_source = WIN_TAG`, all `MANIFEST_COLUMNS` from the win row.
     3. In win ids → `win_failed`: identity columns from the win prepared row, every result column NA.
     4. Otherwise raise `ValueError`, since the universe must be complete.
   - Extra columns at the end:
     - `window_m2`, `outdoor_wall_m2`, `share_achieved`, `fallback` and `source_tree`, from `<D>_win_2026-10-01/windows.csv` by stem;
     - `eui_0908` and `eui_source_0908`, from the base row;
     - `eui_nowin`, the `eui_kwh_m2` of the same building in `<D>_merged_2026-10-01/<slug>_manifest.csv` where that row's `eui_source == "fix_2026-10-01"`, else NA (NA when the file does not exist yet).
   - Counts: `{WIN_TAG, "win_failed"}`.
   - Output folder `_merged_win_2026-10-01`, same files and summary fields as the `2026-10-01` branch.
   - The summary adds `n_win_failed`, `win_failed_ids` and `pooled_eui_kwh_m2`. The pooled EUI is Σ heating / Σ floor over the `WIN_TAG` rows, the same formula as the 09-08 official numbers.
3. `compare_eu11_eui_ratio.py`:
   - New `--base <manifest path>` (default unchanged) and `--base-label` (default `0908`).
   - When `--base` points at a no-window manifest, rows join on `building_id` with base rows whose `eui_source == "fix_2026-10-01"`, and the columns get the label suffix.
   - New column `window_m2_per_floor_m2` from `windows.csv`, when that column is present in the manifest.
   - The reasons of T06d5 apply only when the base is 09-08.
   - For a no-window base, the summary gives:
     - median, p05 and p95 of `heating_ratio`;
     - the Spearman correlation of `heating_ratio` with `window_m2_per_floor_m2`;
     - the count of homes with `window_m2 == 0` whose `heating_ratio` is outside 0.99–1.01;
     - the count with `heating_ratio < 1.0`.
4. Tests in `tests/test_harvest_eu11_win_2026_10_01.py`, no network, monkeypatched as in the fix3 tests:
   - `speed_jobs.txt` is read; a missing file raises;
   - win branch counts on a toy base of 6 rows: 1 undivided, 3 win ok, 1 win failed, and 1 not in win ids, which raises in its own test;
   - a failed row has NA EUI;
   - the `windows.csv` columns are joined by stem;
   - `eui_nowin` is filled only from `fix_2026-10-01` rows.
   Add one toy test in `tests/test_compare_eu11_eui_ratio.py` for `--base` with a no-window base.

**How to test.**
- The new tests pass, and `pytest -k "harvest or compare_eu11" -n 8` passes.
- Dry run of `python scripts/cluster/harvest_eu11_merged.py --district <D> --dry-run --merged-tag win_2026-10-01` for each district. It writes no folder and makes no network call. While the director has not written `speed_jobs.txt` and nothing has been harvested, it reports `win_failed` = 1,172 / 522 / 1,240 / 1,179.
- Dry runs with `2026-10-01`, `2026-09-29` and `2026-09-08` give the same counts as before the edit.
- No ssh, no non-dry harvest.
- Append `#### T06l — windowed harvest code — completed <date>` to §8 and STOP.

**After T06l (director only).**
1. Write `speed_jobs.txt` per district.
2. When the windowed arrays finish: sweep FAILED tasks once (the 10-minute dead-task rule applies), then run the non-dry harvest.
3. Run `compare_eu11_eui_ratio.py` twice: against 09-08, and against the no-window `_merged_2026-10-01` for Madrid, Lyon and London (Bologna once its reference runs finish).
4. **CP-3c**, signed by the director under the standing accuracy ruling. Adopt the windowed set when all of these hold:
   - every home ran, or each failure is named;
   - the homes with `window_m2 == 0` have heating ratio 0.99–1.01 against no-window;
   - heating rises with window area per floor area (Spearman > 0);
   - FINDING 283 is reported with its population.
5. Then write `results/EU11_results_<date>_{per_home,summary}.{csv,json}` in the same form as the 09-08 files. Send 5J the manifest paths for Madrid and Bologna only. Then T07 and T08.

### T06n — Every wall decided by a robust vote, not the centre point (`FINDING 285`, director 2026-10-01 18:00, after the gsscanada-f2 report)

**What.**
1. In `openubem/idf/surface_orientation.py`, replace the WALL decision of `_wall_probe` (lines 79-102). It applies to every wall: outdoor and interzone.
   - **Vote first.** Take the wall base: the vertices within 0.02 m of the wall's minimum z, from the first to the last of them. Use 5 points along the base at 10, 30, 50, 70 and 90 %. Probe each point at 0.02 m and at 0.2 m along the horizontal normal, on both sides, against the zone's FLOOR polygons. Each probe that is inside on exactly one side votes `in` or `out`, for 10 votes at most. The wall is decided when `in` ≠ `out`. Record `how = "vote"`.
   - **Then the floor edge.** Ignore zone floor polygons with |area| < 0.5 m². Find the floor edge that is collinear with the base (both base ends within 0.02 m of the edge line) and has the longest positive overlap. The floor polygon's signed area gives its inside side; the wall is outward when its horizontal normal points away from that side. Record `how = "edge"`.
   - **Last, the zone centroid**, as today. Record `how = "fallback"`.
   - Keep the pair step (rules 1/2/3). Rule 1 now reads "keep the side decided by vote or edge"; `margin` stays for fallback-only pairs.
2. In `scripts/eu21/11_fix_orientation.py`, set `OUT_SUFFIX = "_win_2026-10-03"` and keep `SRC_SUFFIX = "_win_2026-10-01"`. Run `--all` with at most 10 processes. In `orientation.csv`, add columns `walls_vote`, `walls_edge` and `walls_fallback`. Never write under `_win_2026-10-01` or `_win_2026-10-02`.
3. Add unit tests in `tests/test_eu_surface_orientation_2026_10_02.py`:
   - an L-shaped zone where the wall centre probe at 0.2 m is ambiguous but the vote decides it;
   - a zone whose floor list includes a 0.01 m² sliver, where the sliver must not decide;
   - all existing tests stay green.
4. Extend the FINDING 284 entry in `docs/docs_EXPLANATION/OpenUBEM_debug_References.md` ch.1 with one sentence: the centre-point probe with a centroid fallback left L-shaped-zone walls inward (FINDING 285); fixed by the vote.

**Why.** FINDING 285: the 0.2 m centre probe is ambiguous for about 1 % of walls. The centroid fallback is wrong in L-shaped or thin zones, which left ES 261, IT 351, FR 99 and GB 26 outdoor walls inward and flipped ES 8 and IT 6 by mistake. The owner wants highest accuracy before the full run.

**How to test.**
- Director scripts in the scratchpad (`probe.py 0.05`, `edge.py`, `wall_facing.py`, `pairs.py`) on `<D>_win_2026-10-03/idfs`. Gate per district:
  - outdoor walls inward by the 0.05 m centre probe: at most 0.05 % of outdoor walls (list them; a centre point in a notch can disagree with the vote, so these get a director look, not a fail);
  - interzone pairs opposite: N of N;
  - floors down, roofs and ceilings up;
  - window = wall 100 %;
  - geometry changed: 0 homes;
  - `windows.csv` md5 identical to the values in the T06m gate lines.
- Report per district: reversed per type; walls decided by vote, edge and fallback; pair rule 1/2/3 counts; and the re-measure.
- Write the §8 entry `#### T06n — robust wall vote — completed 2026-10-0X`, then STOP. No EnergyPlus, no cluster command.

### T06o — Pair rule 4: the stronger vote wins (director 2026-10-01 17:45, after the T06n stop)

**What.**
1. In `openubem/idf/surface_orientation.py`, make `_wall_probe` also return the vote margin `abs(out_votes - in_votes)` (0 for edge, centroid and degenerate). Keep it in `decided[name]["vote_margin"]`. Callers outside this file that unpack two values must keep working (add a private helper or a third return value and update every caller in the repo; `grep -rn "_wall_probe" openubem scripts tests` first).
2. In the pair step, replace the `else` branch that appends to `pair_rule3` with rule 4: when both sides are vote/edge-decided and the pair is same-direction, the side with the larger `vote_margin` wins and the other side's `flip` is toggled; count it in `result["pair_rule4"]`. Only on an exact tie, append to `pair_rule3` as today.
3. `scripts/eu21/11_fix_orientation.py`: add column `pair_rule4`; keep `OUT_SUFFIX = "_win_2026-10-03"` and rebuild all four `<D>_win_2026-10-03` trees from `_win_2026-10-01` with `--all` (10 local processes max).
4. One unit test: two zones sharing a wall, one side decided 10-0 by vote, the other by edge with the wrong answer; after the run the pair is opposite and the vote side is unchanged.

**Why.** The director measured the 13 T06n stop pairs on the 10-01 files: in 11 the winning side has a clean 10-0 vote and the other side has 0 votes (decided by edge); the other 2 are 4-vs-1 and 4-vs-2. A full vote is the most reliable evidence we have; no pair is a tie.

**How to test (T06o).** Same gate as T06n, plus: pair rule 3 = 0 in every district; interzone pairs opposite N of N; `windows.csv` md5 unchanged from the T06n gate lines; geometry changed 0. Write `#### T06o — pair rule 4 — completed 2026-10-0X`, then STOP. No EnergyPlus, no cluster command.

### T06p — Flat count follows the floor area (`D-EU-141`, `FINDING 286`, owner 2026-10-01: "ok keep going as you continue", answering the D-EU-141 recommendation (a))

**What.**
1. `scripts/run_eu_s2_district_campaign.py` `_geometry` (`:161-173`), imputed branch only (no observed count, building type not SFH/TH): `mean_flat_m2 = rec["geometry"]["a_c_ref_m2"] / rec["n_apartment"]` from the archetype record; `dwellings_val = max(1, round(float(footprint.area) * n_storey / mean_flat_m2))`. If the record or either value is missing, keep today's value. SFH/TH stay 1. Observed counts are untouched except item 2.

When one `catastro_local_id` is joined to more than one footprint with overlap ≥ 5 m2, each footprint gets `max(1, round(n_dwellings * overlap_i / sum(overlap)))`. Add the constants `MIN_CATASTRO_OVERLAP_M2 = 5.0` at module top.

Then assemble `EU-11/<D>_win_2026-10-04/` = `_win_2026-10-03` with the rebuilt homes' IDFs replaced and new `fleet.lst` = the replaced stems only.

Write `#### T06p — flat count follows area — completed 2026-10-0X`, then STOP. No EnergyPlus, no cluster command.

### T06q — Remove degenerate surfaces from every `_win_2026-10-04` IDF (`FINDING 287`, director 2026-10-01 21:00 EDT under the owner's standing rulings "continue however you recommend by considering always highest accuracy" and "ok continue to the end"; requested by 5J for Madrid and Bologna)

**What.**
1. New module `openubem/idf/degenerate_surfaces.py`, one public function `drop_degenerate_surfaces(idf_text_or_obj, tol_m=0.01) -> report`. For each `BUILDINGSURFACE:DETAILED`: drop consecutive vertices closer than `tol_m` (wrap-around included), then drop a vertex whose distance to the line through its two neighbours is under `tol_m`, repeating until stable. A surface left with fewer than 3 vertices is **deleted**, together with (a) every `FENESTRATIONSURFACE:DETAILED` whose `Building_Surface_Name` is that surface, and (b) its partner named in `Outside_Boundary_Condition_Object` when that partner is also degenerate. Surfaces with 3 or more vertices left are **not touched** (no vertex rewrite). Report per surface: name, zone, type, boundary condition, original area (m2), partner name, partner deleted (y/n).
2. **Stop rule:** if a deleted surface's partner is NOT degenerate, do not delete either; list the pair in the report as `partner_not_degenerate` and keep the IDF unchanged for that pair.
3. New step script `scripts/eu21/12_drop_degenerate.py --districts ES,FR,GB,IT --tree _win_2026-10-04`: runs item 1 over every IDF of `EU-11/<D>_win_2026-10-04/idfs/`, rewrites only the IDFs that change (same writer settings as `11_fix_orientation.py`), then rewrites `md5_2026-10-04.txt`, and writes `degenerate_2026-10-04.csv` (stem, n_deleted, total_area_m2, names, boundary types, partner_not_degenerate count). `changed_stems.txt` gets a second column `reason` = `flat_count`, `degenerate` or `flat_count+degenerate`; `fleet.lst` = every stem in `changed_stems.txt`.
4. **Two parts.** Part A (now): items 1, 2, 5, the code of item 3, and the two counter cross-checks below, all read-only on the trees; write `#### T06q part A — completed` and STOP. The T06p item-5 executor is still writing the `_win_2026-10-04` trees, so Part A must not write into any `_win_2026-10-04` folder. Part B (after director go): run item 3 on all four districts and the after-pass checks.
5. Unit tests in `tests/` (the folder used by the T06m tests): a 4-vertex wall with two vertices 5 mm apart and one collinear → deleted with its partner and its window; a valid triangle with one edge 2 cm → untouched; a degenerate wall whose partner is valid → `partner_not_degenerate`, IDF unchanged.

**Why.** EnergyPlus reports `GetSurfaceData: There are N degenerate surfaces` (Severe) on 5J's count for 75 Madrid and 20 Bologna homes of `_win_2026-10-03`, and on 40 of the first 1,201 finished London runs on Speed. EnergyPlus drops these surfaces itself and the run completes, so the energy effect is expected to be near zero; but every such run carries a Severe error, which 5J's clean-run rule rejects and which hides real Severe errors in our own sweeps. Known cause: the 1 mm snap in `openubem/geometry/european_nocore.py:131` is finer than EnergyPlus's ~10 mm vertex tolerance (debug references, chapter 1, the `[OPEN] GetSurfaceData: Very small surface area` entry).

**How to test.**
- Counter cross-check on the untouched `_win_2026-10-03` trees: Madrid and Bologna counts equal 5J's list `C:/Users/o_iseri/Desktop/GSSCanada/GSSCanada-main/5J_docs_occ/Step9_docs/impl/wp9k/degenerate_win3.csv` (md5 99cab93216242c432a26ee661cd63bce): 75 buildings / 738 surfaces and 20 / 59, stem by stem (list every mismatch).
- London cross-check: run `grep -l "degenerate surfaces" out/*/eplusout.err` on Speed in `/nfs/speed-scratch/o_iseri/fleets/EU11_GB-LDN-STDUNSTANS_win_2026-10-03/` (login-node file op only); every stem it lists must be in our London count, and our count's N must equal EnergyPlus's N for each (list mismatches).
- After the pass: our counter finds 0 degenerate surfaces in every `_win_2026-10-04` IDF; `partner_not_degenerate` count per district reported; total deleted area per district (m2) and the largest single deleted area reported (expected under 0.01 m2 each).
- Stems not in `changed_stems.txt` are byte-identical to `_win_2026-10-03` (md5 compare, N of N).
- The T06o orientation gate still passes on every IDF the pass changed.
- Write `#### T06q — degenerate surfaces — completed 2026-10-0X` in §8 with the counts per district and reason column totals, then STOP. No EnergyPlus, no cluster command except the London grep above.

The 10-01 file is not changed.

6. New columns on `win_2026-10-05` rows: `dwellings_used`, `dwellings_source` (from `prepared_buildings.csv`). Other columns as `win_2026-10-01`.
7. New script `scripts/cluster/export_flat_counts_4j.py --district <D>` (GB and IT only; it refuses ES and FR). It writes `<D>_merged_win_2026-10-05/flat_counts_2026-10-05.csv` with `building_id,old_count,new_count,dwellings_source,status`: one row per building of `openubem/outputs/3D/eu_<D>_data/buildings.csv`; `old_count,new_count,dwellings_source` from `<D>_win_2026-10-05/dwelling_counts_2026-10-05.csv` (empty where the building has no row there); `status` = `simulated` (row with `eui_source == win_2026-10-05` in the merged manifest), `run_failed` (`win_failed`), `undivided_excluded` (in the 10-05 undivided list), `not_residential` (`class == excluded`), `no_model` (any other). Print the count per status.
8. Tests in a new `tests/test_harvest_eu11_win_2026_10_05.py`: synthetic tree with a 3-stem `fleet.lst` and 5 IDFs gives 5 rows; a `speed_jobs.txt` line `"1410433 oom_resubmit_32G idx=5"` gives job 1410433; an md5 mismatch raises; a stem with no `_dwelling_` zone is listed as undivided; the export maps each of the five statuses. `tests/test_harvest_eu11_win_2026_10_01.py` stays green.

**Why.** The 10-05 fleets are the ones running now. London's run list is partial by design, its job file has a resubmit line, the area-based flat count turned 22 more homes into undivided ones (owner rule D-EU-139: undivided homes are left out), and `prepared_buildings.csv` carries stale IDF digests (B1 deviation 3). 4J was promised the per-building file with a status column.

**How to test.** Unit tests (item 8). Dry runs (no network), each with the measured counts: `--merged-tag win_2026-10-05 --dry-run` for all four districts gives rows = base 09-08 rows − undivided (ES 1,187 − 23, FR 529 − 9, GB 1,240 − 0, IT 1,215 − 48), and every remaining base id is in the 10-05 `idfs/` (else the existing `ValueError`). Dry run of `--merged-tag win_2026-10-01` gives the same counts as the T06l entry. Write `#### T06r — harvest code for win_2026-10-05 — completed 2026-10-02`, then STOP. No ssh, no non-dry harvest, no EnergyPlus.

## 7. Stop-and-report points

- **CP-1 (after T01).** Director reads the census; confirms or trims the scope of T02–T04 (e.g. drops U3 if `u3_causes.csv` shows only data-driven cases). No code before CP-1.
- **CP-1b (after T04c).** Owner, 2026-09-29, verbatim: "why we pass to the simualions, we need to solve these". T05 and T06 are on hold. Director writes fix tasks only for verdicts a cutter or pair repair can reach (`A`, `C`, `D`, `same_polygon_extra_vertex`, `one_side_larger`); `B_rule_impossible` and `E_far` go to the owner as one decision (keep best effort, or change the frozen rule). No simulation before the fix tasks close.
- **CP-1c (after T04d + T04e).** Owner, 2026-09-29, verbatim: "continue to the end lets go". Director audits both, runs the T04d EnergyPlus proof on Speed, then goes straight into T05 when both are clean (0 regressions, 0 proof fatals). The homes that stay unfixable (`k1_hole`, `k1_lobed`, `rule_impossible_c6`, `still_far`) keep their current best-effort or one-zone form and go to the owner as one decision at CP-3.
- **CP-1c amended (director, 2026-09-29 23:55).** T04d recovered 30 of 92 and left the OK-100 check at 96 of 100, so CP-1c is not clean: T04f closes the loose ends, then the director runs the T04f proof on Speed, then T05. The T04d proof of the 30 is on Speed now.
- **CP-1c amended again (director, 2026-09-30 00:10).** T04f recovered 44 of 92 and traced the OK-100 change to the OPEN-66 guard; the T04d proof still shows 1 fatal (ring spike). T04g fixes both; then the director runs the T04g proof on Speed, then T05.
- **CP-2 (after T05).** Before-class → after-class table, regression gate result, Speed case count. Director tells 4J the populations that will move, then ships. Owner ruling 2026-09-29, verbatim: "yes, start without stopping if clean" — a clean gate (0 regressions, every candidate re-emitted) passes CP-2 straight into T06; any regression or failed re-emission still stops here.
- **CP-3 (after T06).** Owner decides whether the new district EUIs replace the 2026-09-08 figures. Until then nothing published changes.
- **CP-3 ruled (owner, 2026-09-30, "i recommend yes").** T06 EUIs not adopted. T06c → director audit (envelope gate clean: hidden area 0 and envelope within 1 % on every rebuilt home, or each exception named) → T06d → director ships the `_fix_2026-09-30` fleets on Speed and harvests into `_merged_2026-09-30` → **CP-3b**: owner decides adoption, with the envelope gate and the per-building EUI ratio vs 09-08 shown.
- **Standing owner ruling (2026-09-30, verbatim: "ok continue continue however you recommend by considering always highest accuracy").** The director signs CP-3b itself: adopt `_merged_2026-09-30` if the envelope gate is clean and every EUI ratio outside 0.8–1.25 vs 09-08 is explained; otherwise keep 09-08 and name the blocker. If the T06c scan finds hidden outdoor area in the 3,874 non-candidates, the most accurate route is taken (rebuild and re-run them on Speed as T06e) and the owner is told.
- **CP-3 amended (director, 2026-10-01).** T06c audit: gate (3b) failed, cause = flats overlapping on a storey (`FINDING 278`), also in 85 published homes. `_fix_2026-09-30` not shipped. T06e items 1–4 → **CP-3a** (director audits the 20-home trial) → T06e item 5 → director audit of the full gate → T06d (amended) → Speed → **CP-3b** as above, the owner told that the published set changes beyond the 297. *Amended again (CP-3a, `D-EU-135`):* trial clean on overlap but upper storeys lose floor (stacked storey-0 outlines, `FINDING 279`, ~1,770 published homes) → T06e item 3(d) + 4b re-trial on 30 homes → **CP-3a again** → item 5 on all 4,160 homes → as above.

Three implementation choices the director may want to know: (1) a wing bend is a cut-made inside corner whose party-wall run (the chain of non-boundary edges through the corner) ends within 0.10 m of a reflex vertex of the plate outline, the plate being the union of the floor's zones with mitre joins so corners are not rounded; (2) `c13_bent_walls` and `wing_bends` are the maximum over floors of the per-floor count of such corners; (3) `u3_causes.csv` re-runs `cut_storey_nocore` on the raw `02_residential_manifest.gpkg` footprint (after `_drop_redundant_ring_vertices`) at each distinct per-storey dwelling count from `allocate_european_dwellings`, because a fallback building keeps no cut.

#### T04d — Repair the paired surfaces that still send 92 homes to one zone per floor (`D-EU-122`) — completed 2026-09-29

- **Artifacts:** `scripts/run_eu_s2_campaign.py` (new `_plane_basis`, `_repair_interzone_pairs`, `_mirror_near_same_group`, `_split_larger_group`, `_find_remainder_partner`, `_clean_surface_rings`, constants `MIRROR_NEAR_SAME_AREA_M2 = 0.01`, `MAX_ADIABATIC_REMAINDER_M2 = 1.0`, `RING_CLEAN_MIN_EDGE_M = 0.01`; `_mirror_interzone_pairs_stats` / `_mirror_interzone_pairs` kept as wrappers; `build_idf_for_building` now runs repair, `_drop_sliver_surfaces`, `_clean_surface_rings`); `tests/test_eu_floor_division_residual_2026_09_29.py` (6 new tests); `docs/docs_EXPLANATION/OpenUBEM_debug_References.md` (3 entries); scratch `t04d_rebuild.py`, `u1e/`, `u1e_result.csv`, `u1e_ep_list.txt` (30 lines), `ok100e/`, `ok100e_result.csv`. Engine code untouched, no EnergyPlus, no cluster, at most 5 local processes.
- **Deviations:** (1) `RING_CLEAN_MIN_EDGE_M = 0.01` is new and `_clean_ring_3d` now uses it; the frozen `NEAR_DUPLICATE_VERTEX_TOLERANCE_M = 0.005` and the at-risk detector are untouched. Needed for the two mismatch fatals: both rings were already exact reverses (8 and 8 vertices) but carried a 5.1 to 5.4 mm edge, just over 5 mm, which EnergyPlus collapses asymmetrically. (2) `_split_larger_group` handles groups of 3 or more (one large surface over several smaller ones, each smaller one re-paired to its own copy of the large one, name suffix `_S<n>`), not only pairs; the plan wording covers pairs only, but 115 of the skipped groups have 3 to 5 members. (3) The remainder is `L` minus the smaller surfaces buffered by 0.005 m (mitre join), so edge strips under 5 mm are not counted. (4) A candidate partner for a remainder piece must lie within 0.01 m of the piece's plane, face the other way, and hold no window. (5) `_clean_surface_rings` skips members of a group that stays unresolved, and skips a ring whose cleaned form has no plane (geomeppy `setcoords` raised `ZeroDivisionError` on 3 buildings before this guard). (6) Degenerate groups (item 3) are left to the existing `_drop_sliver_surfaces` call that follows the repair; no separate code.
- **Test status:** new file 20 of 20 pass (extra-vertex pair mirrored; remainder dropped, adiabatic, paired; over-cap pair unchanged; doubled floor vertex removed); with `test_eu_collinear_ring_cleanup.py` 31 of 31 pass; `test_eu_recut_95pct_2026_09_08.py` 18 of 19 pass, the 19th (`test_t02_dry_run_best_effort_measurement`) did not finish in 40 minutes and was stopped (layout-only test on a machine loaded by T04e; not exercised by this change). Rebuild of the 92 (4 processes, 0 errors): recovered (`at_risk` False) 30 of 92 (Madrid 13 of 33, Lyon 2 of 10, London 3 of 9, Bologna 12 of 40); still rerouted 62 of 92 (Madrid 20, Lyon 8, London 6, Bologna 28; 49 `interzone_vertex_mismatch`, 13 `near_duplicate_vertex`). Groups over the 92: seen 3,581; coverage failed 452; near-same mirrored 263; split 24 (remainder pieces: 9 paired, 9 dropped, 0 adiabatic); still skipped 165; ring cleanings 1,866; sliver surfaces removed 1,544. The 4 fatal proof homes: 4 of 4 recovered with 0 doubled-vertex surfaces and 0 mismatched pairs at the gate. OK-100 (seed 20260929, IDF compared object by object is not possible after T04e's engine edits): `at_risk` False on 96 of 100, 1 of 100 not built, 3 of 100 rerouted; zone count equal to the T02b run on 96 of 100; 1 of 99 had a surface changed by this task (5 rings cleaned; 2 groups near-same mirrored across 99 buildings). `u1e_ep_list.txt` = 30 IDFs; EnergyPlus proof left to the director.

Madrid `803d0afdbbbb83e9` and `c5e730d9efda6799` (7 vs 6, 14 vs 15 vertices): the mirror saw exact reverses and skipped the group, and `_clean_ring_3d` kept a 5.1 to 5.4 mm edge (28 and 84 such edges on interzone rings); fixed by the 0.01 m edge limit in `_clean_ring_3d`.
- **The 62 still rerouted:** 51 carry at least one group the plan's rules leave alone: 139 of the 165 skipped groups have a remainder over the 1.0 m2 adiabatic cap (110 groups of 3 or more members, 29 pairs; e.g. Madrid `way/942352870` pairs a 54.72 m2 ceiling with a 3-vertex floor of 0.0 m2, so nothing above it exists to pair with), 23 groups of 3 or more have a remainder under 1 m2 but a smaller member facing the same side as the large one or overlapping another, and 3 pairs are partial overlaps (symmetric difference 0.01 m2 exactly on the boundary of the rule). The other 11 have no skipped group: reason `near_duplicate_vertex`, tripping surface is a `Ceiling` of storey 0 that is a sliver triangle of 0.0015 to 0.003 m2 (Madrid `c7a2ddbf4f24215f`, `2caab5144dc387d3`, `39b9e0d4f9f62538`; Lyon `cd79f626ce3e79fa`, `a585de39a07bcd04`, `93647659bf673ce1`, `a91391a979249824`; London `6e806ec067d0fc74`; Bologna `2b1ea068842e0aba`, `cc7188fda56e6408`, `738ea747c3d1db30`); `_drop_sliver_surfaces` removed nothing because the building's sliver total exceeds its 0.1 m2 limit (measured on `39b9e0d4f9f62538`: 64 surfaces, 0.128 m2, largest 0.003 m2). Not changed: that limit is `D-EU-121`, outside this task.

- **Notes:** 🛑 STOP after item 4, item 5 not run. **Finding for the director:** the clip removes the overlap but also removes floor area on upper storeys. Per-storey floor union vs the 09-08 IDF is below 0.99 on 17 of 20 homes (min 0.394; e.g. `way/298850489` storeys 1-3 860 of 2,441 m2, Bologna `28969` storeys 4-5 52 of 864 m2, Madrid `way/435397050` storeys 2-3 89 of 275 m2). Cause: `_extrude_zone_groups` (`openubem/idf/surfaces.py`, `add_block(coordinates=group[0]["coords_m"], num_stories=n)`) stacks the first storey's polygon of every dwelling label through all storeys, so on upper storeys a label's surfaces cover its storey-0 polygon, not its own polygon there; clipping to the own polygon cuts away the wrong part and nothing covers the rest. G1 cannot see this; G3 sees only the roof (7 of 20 fail). The published homes (09-08) never had this loss (their union equals the footprint, with overlap on top). Option (a) alone is therefore not enough for item 5: the extrusion has to take each storey's own polygon (a change in frozen `surfaces.py` or a replacement of `_extrude_zone_groups` from `run_eu_s2_campaign.py`), then the clip is a safety net. Director rules before item 5.

#### T06f — director audit, IDF swap on Speed, FAILED resubmit — 2026-10-01
- **Artifacts:** Speed `EU11_<D>_fix_2026-10-01/idfs/` updated in place (tar → `t06f_tmp/` → `mv`, temp removed); resubmit jobs `1405950` (Lyon, idx 44), `1405951` (London, 23 idx), `1405952` (Madrid, 11 idx); `out/<stem>` of the 35 resubmitted stems removed by name first (the sbatch reuses a stale `expanded.idf` if ExpandObjects writes none).
- **Audit (director, measured):** gate csv tallies per district — preflight_new > 0: 0/0/0/0; zones unequal: 0/0/0/0; `_PREFLIGHT_FALLBACK`: 0/0/0/0; floor not ok: Madrid 0, Lyon 1 (`d05053aee5953e2b`, shapely union on two self-intersecting rings, Newell area moves 0.002 m² — accepted), London 0, Bologna 0; `changed.lst` = sha_changed rows (215/104/98/295), all ⊂ fleet.lst; outcome labels unchanged on every home; schedules of every swapped stem identical to 09-08 build. Every FAILED stem of the three running jobs at 12:24 (Lyon 1, London 23, Madrid 11 = 35) is in `changed.lst`. md5 of two swapped files equal local ↔ Speed.
- **Swap rule:** only changed stems that had not started (Lyon idx > 72, Madrid idx > 132, all Bologna) or had FAILED: Lyon 66, London 23, Madrid 135, Bologna 295 = 519 IDFs. COMPLETED and RUNNING stems keep the IDF they ran (sbatch copies the IDF at task start, `submit_recut.sbatch:52`). Changed stems that complete on the old IDF are valid runs (vertex cleanup only, Newell area unchanged); any that FAIL later get the next resubmit with the new IDF, which is already on disk only for not-started stems — director re-swaps on the next FAILED sweep.
- **Deviations accepted:** 2-line `_PREFLIGHT_FALLBACK` suffix edit in `scripts/run_eu_s2_district_campaign.py`; `degenerate_removed` / `degenerate_area_m2` absent from pass-stats csv (not load-bearing). F3 exceptions (153 unflagged homes whose rings lost spike/duplicate/collinear vertices, no surface dropped) accepted under the same Newell-area argument.
- **Test status:** executor 86/86 on the subset run; ceiling82 tests not run (may call EnergyPlus).
- **Next:** at each FAILED sweep, swap remaining changed IDFs for the failed stems and resubmit; then T06d harvest.

- **Cause (item 1):** `idf.intersect_match()` inside `extrude_geometry` (`openubem/idf/surfaces.py:928`, frozen) writes some `_N` fragments of facing ceiling/floor pieces with reversed vertex order. Measured on `a2c94661a5028bb1`: 0 wrongly wound horizontals after `_extrude_zone_groups_per_storey` (`run_eu_s2_campaign.py:1080`), 48 after `extrude_geometry`; the clip, `_clean_surface_rings` and `_remove_degenerate_surfaces` do not create or change them. - **Gate (N of M):** (H1) `EXPOSED_BETWEEN_ZONES` and whole preflight = [] on 40 of 40 rebuilt; the token flags 40 of 40 on their `_t06f` IDF. (H2) floor sum within max(0.1 %, 0.5 m2) and zone count equal: 40 of 40; `_PREFLIGHT_FALLBACK` 0 of 40. (H3) 40 controls (10 per district, seed 20261001): `idf_sha256` equal to `_t06f`, 40 of 40, 0 exceptions. - **Deviations:** (1) Sign. Plan item 2 says Floor Newell z < 0, Roof/Ceiling z > 0. With `_newell_normal` every healthy fleet IDF has Floor z > 0 and Roof/Ceiling z < 0 (three sample homes: 94 of 94 floors, 92 of 92 roofs/ceilings; its wall normals also point inward), so the literal sign would flip every floor of every home and fail H3. Implemented as constant `FLOOR_NORMAL_Z_SIGN = +1` (floor z must be > 0, roof/ceiling z < 0); it flips exactly the two wrong surfaces of each pair, same set the director scan names. #### FINDING 282 — no windows in any EU district model — director — 2026-10-01 15:22 EDT
- Found while answering the GSSCanada 5J questions (Madrid + Bologna only). Owner: "why? we need to add basic window-to-wall-ratio, as standard one".
- Measured: 0 window objects (`FENESTRATIONSURFACE:DETAILED`, `WINDOW`, `WINDOWMATERIAL:SIMPLEGLAZINGSYSTEM`) in every IDF of `EU-11/<D>_recut_2026-09-08` (1,187 / 529 / 1,240 / 1,215), `<D>_fix_2026-10-01` (1,183 / 529 / 1,239 / 1,209), and Madrid `nocore_2026-09-03` (952), `full_fleet_2026-09-04` (1,008), `final_2026-09-07` (1,175). Each IDF has 4 `CONSTRUCTION` objects (wall, roof, floor, internal mass), none for glazing.
- Cause: `build_idf_for_building` (`scripts/run_eu_s2_campaign.py:1843`) assigns only wall / roof / floor constructions (`_envelope_construction` ×3, `_assign_envelope_constructions`); there is no window step. Only the single-box S0 model `openubem/idf/european_box.py:189-247` places TABULA windows. No EU decision record says to leave them out: a missing step, not a choice.
- Data already in the library: `openubem/data/construction/tabula_archetypes_{es,fr,gb,it}.json` `records[]` → `geometry.a_window_components_m2`, `geometry.a_window_by_orientation_m2`, `geometry.a_wall_components_m2`, `u_components_w_m2k.window`, `u_window_w_m2k`, `g_gl_window`. Window / (window + wall) median: ES 0.14 (0.00–0.52, 24 records), IT 0.10 (0.06–0.18, 42 records).
- Wall shapes (Madrid / Bologna fix IDFs, outdoor walls): 4-vertex vertical rectangles 36,564 / 58,473; triangles 734 / 1,079; other 4-vertex 3 / 10; homes with one or more non-rectangle outdoor wall 114 / 161.
- Effect: every published EU EUI (09-08) and the 10-01 re-run in flight are opaque-envelope only: no glazing heat loss (TABULA window U is several times the wall U) and no solar gain through glass. Direction and size of the net bias not measured.
- Also corrected to 5J: air change is modelled (one `ZONEVENTILATION:DESIGNFLOWRATE` per zone, AirChanges/Hour), not absent.
- Open: owner decision `D-EU-140` (how to set the window ratio), then plan task, rebuild, Speed re-run. The 10-01 runs keep going as the geometry crash sweep.

#### FINDING 284 — every outdoor wall in every EU model faces into its own room (director, 2026-10-01 16:20 EDT)

- **Reported by** the 5J session (Madrid/Bologna only); **re-measured by the director on all four districts** before acting.
- **Measure** (`scratchpad/wall_facing.py`): Newell normal of each outdoor wall's vertex order; a point 0.2 m along it from the wall centre tested against the zone's floor polygons. Windowed fleets `<D>_win_2026-10-01`: ES 35,709 inward / 11 outward / 463 unclear (1,161 homes mostly inward, 11 ties); FR 19,682 / 23 / 216 (519, 3); GB 13,414 / 6 / 67 (1,235, 5); IT 55,234 / 8 / 491 (1,108, 71). Ties = 11/3/5/71 = exactly the FINDING 283 homes with no outdoor wall. Every window has the same normal as its wall.
- **Published 09-08 models too:** 40 Madrid `recut_2026-09-08` IDFs: 1,183 inward / 0 outward / 21 unclear.
- **EnergyPlus keeps the wrong direction:** Madrid 0275c53572b2ff9f (fix run, `eplusout.sql` Surfaces table): Wall 0001 azimuth 304.36°, equal to the vertex-order normal (304.4°), which points into the zone; the real outward facing is ~124°. eplusout.err auto-fixes upside-down floors/roofs only, nothing for walls. So opaque-wall solar (all published EUIs) and window solar (the windowed fleet) come from the opposite side.
- **Consequence:** the full windowed submission is HELD (40-home sample left to finish, it is cheap). The 09-08 official EUIs and the no-window 10-01 reference carry wall solar from the wrong side; size unknown until re-run.
- **Likely source (not yet confirmed):** the EU IDF writer (`scripts/run_eu_s2_campaign.py`, `orient(part, 1.0)` at :481 and `_fix_horizontal_orientation` at :783) — a fix task must find where the ring order is reversed relative to `GlobalGeometryRules CounterClockWise`.
- Next free D-EU-141 / FINDING 285.
- **Root cause (director, 16:45 EDT):** `scripts/run_eu_s2_campaign.py:1806` orients every zone footprint counter-clockwise (`ZONE_WINDING_SIGN = 1.0`, :72) before geomeppy `add_block`. geomeppy's own orientation check is broken (`is_clockwise()` always False, debug-references ch.1), so a CCW footprint comes out fully reversed. Measured across the four windowed fleets: floors point up (ES 3,617/3,617 ground floors up), ceilings and roofs point down, walls point in. Exceptions are few: IT has 9 floor/ceiling pairs already the right way (probably the T06g repair), ES has 1 floor and 1 roof outdoors already the right way, GB has 2 floors and 1 roof. So a blind reversal of everything is wrong for those; the fix must decide surface by surface, from a measurement.
- **Same signature outside the EU arc (unmeasured, do not quote):** debug-references ch.1 records "upside down" on 8,160/8,160 North American `auto` buildings and "winding itself is still unchanged". The adopted North American fleet (171.38) probably has inward walls too. That needs its own measurement in the openings arc, not this plan.
- **Side note from 5J (Madrid/Bologna):** their author ruled "yes of course" to fixing at source. That is NOT this session's owner ruling. T06m below waits for our owner.

### T06m — Point every surface the right way, surface by surface (`FINDING 284`, owner 2026-10-01 ~17:00: "once you fix, just cancel existing simulations, clean them and re-run simulations walls fixed and with windows. of course fix them with windows models")

**What.**
1. Add `openubem/idf/surface_orientation.py` with `normalise_surface_orientation(idf) -> dict`, which edits each `BUILDINGSURFACE:DETAILED` in place:
   - FLOOR: normal must point down (z<0).
   - CEILING and ROOF: normal must point up (z>0).
   - WALL: normal must point away from its own zone. Test a point 0.2 m along the horizontal normal from the wall centre against the zone's FLOOR polygons, and the same 0.2 m on the other side. If neither side or both sides are inside, use the sign of normal·(wall centre − zone floor centroid).
   - A surface that fails its rule gets its vertex list reversed.
   - Each `FENESTRATIONSURFACE:DETAILED` is reversed exactly when its host surface was reversed.
   - Returns counts: reversed, kept, and decided-by-fallback, for each surface type.
2. Check interzone pairs after the pass. For every `Outside_Boundary_Condition == Surface` pair, the two normals must be opposite (dot < 0). If a pair is not, it is a stop-and-report, not a silent fix.
3. New `scripts/eu21/11_fix_orientation.py --district <D> | --all` (at most 10 local processes, no EnergyPlus). It applies the function to every IDF of `<D>_win_2026-10-01/idfs/` (windowed only; the no-window fleets are retired by the owner ruling) and writes `EU-11/<D>_win_2026-10-02/`: `idfs/`, plus `schedules/`, `weather/`, `fleet.lst`, `prepared_buildings.csv` and `windows.csv` copied unchanged from `_win_2026-10-01`, plus `orientation.csv` (per home: the counts plus the wall inward/outward/unclear re-measure). Report the md5 of each `windows.csv`. Never write under `_win_2026-10-01`.
4. In `build_idf_for_building` (`scripts/run_eu_s2_campaign.py`), call the function right BEFORE `add_tabula_windows`, so future builds come out right and windows copy an already-correct wall order.
5. Add an `[OPEN]`→fixed entry to `docs/docs_EXPLANATION/OpenUBEM_debug_References.md` ch.1.

**Why.** FINDING 284: EnergyPlus keeps wall and window azimuths 180° off, so wall and window solar arrives from the opposite side. Floors and roofs are only right today because EnergyPlus auto-flips them.

**How to test.**
- Unit tests: a CCW box and a CW box both come out with floors down, roof up and walls out. A wall with a window keeps the window normal equal to the wall normal. An interzone floor/ceiling pair stays opposite.
- Re-measure with the director's script `C:/Users/o_iseri/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-o-iseri-Desktop-OpenUBEM/3f02e930-9e86-498a-9aa6-21d54457bb5c/scratchpad/wall_facing.py <D>_win_2026-10-02/idfs ...` (run from `openubem/outputs/eu_evidence/EU-11`): outdoor walls inward at most 0.1 % in every district (unclear ones listed); floors all down; ceilings and roofs all up; window normal = wall normal for 100 %.
- No geometry is lost: per-surface area and vertex sets are unchanged (only the order changes).
- The executor writes the list of IDFs and stops. The director submits a 40-home Speed sample: 0 "upside down" warnings, and the wall azimuth in the SQL Surfaces table equals the measured outward azimuth.
- Tests go in a new `tests/test_eu_surface_orientation_2026_10_02.py`; `tests/test_eu_windows_2026_10_01.py` and `tests/test_eu_floor_division_residual_2026_09_29.py` stay green.
- Write the §8 entry `#### T06m — surface orientation — completed 2026-10-02` with per-district gate lines (homes written N of N; surfaces reversed per type; interzone pairs opposite N of N; re-measure counts; windows.csv md5), then STOP. No EnergyPlus, no cluster command.
- **Cancel + clean (director, 2026-10-01 ~17:15 EDT, owner: "now you stop current runs no need to wait"):** scancel 1405481, 1405559, 1406674, 1407033, 1407035 (the rest had already finished; WorkDir checked = EU11 fleets). Deleted every `EU11_<D>_win_2026-10-01/out` (33 finished + 7 unfinished sample homes, inward walls). In `_fix_2026-10-01/out`, deleted the 56 unfinished folders (FR 3, IT 53); finished no-window results kept untouched as an old reference (ES 746, FR 246, GB 191, IT 88); they carry inward walls and must not be quoted. Watchers stopped. 5J jobs and `wp9d_hh` untouched.
- **Why the walls went wrong (for the record):** (1) geomeppy's own direction check is broken, so it never corrects a footprint's turning direction; (2) in Aug 2026 the "upside-down floor" warning was fixed by forcing footprints counter-clockwise (`ZONE_WINDING_SIGN = 1.0`); that choice was never verified against wall directions, and with this geomeppy it builds every box inside-out; (3) EnergyPlus silently flips floors and roofs and gives no warning for walls, so the warnings looked handled; (4) every geometry check used area, volume or vertex sets, which are identical when only the vertex order is reversed (debug-references ch.1 already warns: "No area- or volume-based geometry check can detect this class of defect"). No test checked a wall's facing direction until 5J did.

#### FINDING 285 — T06m left a few hundred outdoor walls inward (director, 2026-10-01 17:50 EDT)
- Reported by gsscanada-f2 (Madrid and Bologna only); director reproduced it on all four districts.
- Cause: `surface_orientation._wall_probe` (`openubem/idf/surface_orientation.py:79-102`). When the 0.2 m probe gives "unclear", the code falls back to the zone centroid, and that is wrong for L-shaped or thin zones.
- Exact test (scratchpad `edge.py`): match the wall base to its zone floor edge and read the side from the floor's signed area. On the `_win_2026-10-01` walls (before the fix), almost every outdoor wall was inward: 35,653 inward vs 4 outward among clear ES probes.
- Wrong walls in `_win_2026-10-02`, i.e. left unreversed although inward, plus flipped by mistake although they were already outward:

  | District | Left unreversed | Flipped by mistake |
  |---|---|---|
  | ES | 261 | 8 |
  | IT | 351 | 6 |
  | FR | 99 | 0 |
  | GB | 26 | 0 |

- Wall area involved: about 0.03–0.11 % of outdoor wall area.
- Remedy (T06n, to plan): make the floor-edge test the main rule, with the probe at 0.2 m then 0.05 m as fallback and the centroid as the last resort. Rebuild as `_win_2026-10-03`, re-audit and re-stage. Sample jobs 1407370–73 stay useful for the error and warning gate only.
- Also open from 5J: tiny flats. 22 ES and 55 IT buildings have a mean flat area under 15 m², so the source of the dwelling count needs checking.

#### FINDING 286 — Some Madrid and Bologna homes get impossibly small flats (director, 2026-10-01 17:55 EDT)

- **Symptom:** 77 buildings have a mean flat area under 15 m2: Madrid (ES) 22 of 1,172, Bologna (IT) 55 of 1,179. Example: Madrid `30c2677bd279e1bb`, 448 m2 cut into 78 flats.
- **Cause 1 — archetype default applied to any size:** when no observed count exists, `scripts/run_eu_s2_district_campaign.py:161-173` uses the TABULA reference building's `n_apartment` (ES.AB.06 = 78, IT.MidClim.AB.05 = 40; `openubem/data/construction/tabula_archetypes_{es,it}.json`) whatever the footprint size. Bologna has no observed count at all (`observed_dwellings = None` at :634, :1017), so 1,123 Bologna buildings carry the archetype default; 56 SFH/TH get 1.
- **Cause 2 — Catastro count joined to slivers (ES):** the sidecar join `LARGEST_OVERLAP_INTERSECTS_EPSG3035` gives a whole Catastro building count to the OSM footprint with the largest overlap, even 0.29 m2. 94 of 1,172 ES footprints join on under 5 m2; 95 Catastro ids are shared by 2–3 footprints. 13 of the 16 observed-count tiny-flat buildings share an id (median overlap 0.26 m2).
- **Split of the 77:** ES 16 from Catastro (11 AB, 4 MFH, 1 SFH) + 6 archetype default (AB); IT 55 archetype default (34 AB, 21 MFH).
- **Not affected:** Lyon and London (own count sources; not traced here). **Corrected 2026-10-01 19:30 (director):** wrong for London. `_gb_row_outcome` sets `observed_dwellings = None` (`run_eu_s2_district_campaign.py:410`), so London blocks of flats are imputed like Bologna and T06p changes them too; the dry count must report GB and FR changes. 4J (gsscanada-9f) told: `messages_GSSCanada/2026-10-01_OpenUBEM_to_4J_neighbourhood_setup_what_to_repeat.md`. **Corrected again 2026-10-01 20:10 (director):** wrong for Lyon too; the dry count shows 486 of 530 Lyon homes change (no observed count in the FR rows).
- **Remedy:** pending owner decision D-EU-141 (recommend: imputed count = conditioned area ÷ the archetype reference building's mean flat area; Catastro joins under 5 m2 overlap or on a shared id treated as missing). The full EnergyPlus run goes ahead on the T06o files with Bologna queued last; the affected buildings are rebuilt and re-run once the rule is chosen.
- **Source:** tiny-flat tracer report 2026-10-01 (director dispatch).

Counted on `_win_2026-10-03`: Madrid 75 homes / 738 surfaces and Bologna 20 / 59 (5J counter, matched to EnergyPlus on a sample); London 40 of the first 1,201 finished runs on Speed. Lyon not yet counted.

- **Action.** T06q removes them from every `_win_2026-10-04` IDF before staging. Madrid job 1407631 put on hold (21:00 EDT) so it does not start on the old models; release after the ES swap.

#### T06q — director audit of part A, and amendment A2 (director, 2026-10-01 21:40 EDT)

- **Audit:** tests pass, no 10-04 folder touched, 0 `partner_not_degenerate`, Bologna 20/59 exact. Two misses: (1) London per-stem N differs from EnergyPlus on 16 of 40 stems, in both directions (7 stems: ours lower, so EnergyPlus would still flag them after Part B); (2) a degenerate window in Madrid is out of scope. The counter is not EnergyPlus's rule, so Part B is **not** given go yet.
- **EnergyPlus 23.1 rule, read from source** (`src/EnergyPlus/SurfaceGeometry.cc` at tag v23.1.0, raw GitHub). It applies to every surface that goes through `GetVertices`, windows included:
  1. **Coincident pass (:9474-9525).** Walk the vertex ring, wrap-around included. At the first `distance(v_i, v_next) < 0.01` with sides > 3, erase `v_next` and restart the walk. When sides == 3, each edge shorter than 0.01 adds **+1** to the degenerate count and is not erased; the walk then ends.
  2. **Collinear pass (`CheckConvexity`, :15656-15860).** Project onto XY, or XZ, or YZ: the first plane whose `|Σ(a_n b_{n+1} − a_{n+1} b_n)| > 1e-4`. For each n, V1 = p(n+1) − p(n) and V2 = p(n+2) − p(n+1), skipping any with length ≤ 1e-8. θ = asin(cross(V1,V2) / (|V1||V2|)). If |θ| ≤ 1e-6, vertex n+1 is collinear. If sides − collinear count < 3, add **+1** to the degenerate count.
  3. The `GetSurfaceData: There are N degenerate surfaces` N is the sum of these increments over the IDF. It counts events, not surfaces: one surface can add more than 1.
  4. EnergyPlus does **not** delete these surfaces; it keeps them as tiny 3-sided surfaces ("These surfaces should be deleted"). So deleting them removes only their sliver area.
- **Amendment A2 (replaces item 1's detection rule and item 5's first test):**
  - `drop_degenerate_surfaces` uses exactly rules 1–2 above to flag a surface, with no other tolerance. It also returns the EnergyPlus event count per surface.
  - Scope is `BUILDINGSURFACE:DETAILED` and `FENESTRATIONSURFACE:DETAILED`. A flagged window is deleted on its own, and its host wall is untouched.
  - The deletion and partner rules of items 1–2 are unchanged.
  - Tests:
    - A quad with one 5 mm edge and one 5 mm edge in the triangle left gives 1 surface flagged with 1 event.
    - A triangle with two 5 mm edges gives 2 events.
    - A quad that becomes collinear gives 1 event.
    - A valid triangle with a 2 cm edge is untouched.
    - A flagged 9 mm window is deleted and its host is kept.
- **A2 gates (read-only on `_win_2026-10-03`, as in part A):**
  - (a) London: the per-stem event sum equals EnergyPlus's N on all 40 flagged stems, and our list holds no stem that EnergyPlus did not flag among the 1,238 finished. This is the load-bearing check.
  - (b) Madrid and Bologna: the flagged surface set compared with 5J's list. Report mismatches; do not chase them if (a) passes.
  - (c) Lyon: count only.
  - (d) Part A's three tests are replaced by the A2 tests. All pass, together with the orientation tests.
  - Write `#### T06q part A2 — completed`, then STOP. Part B still needs director go.


#### T06q part A2 — completed 2026-10-01

- **Artifacts:** `openubem/idf/degenerate_surfaces.py` rewritten to EnergyPlus 23.1 rules 1-2 (coincident walk `:9474-9525`, `CheckConvexity` `:15656-15860`); public `drop_degenerate_surfaces(idf_text_or_obj, apply=True)` (the `tol_m` argument is gone) plus `energyplus_events(pts, arrays=None)`; report rows carry `kind` (surface/window) and `events`; report adds `n_events`, `n_events_shading`, `n_events_total`. `tests/test_eu_degenerate_surfaces_2026_10_01.py` replaced (8 tests). `scripts/eu21/12_drop_degenerate.py` unchanged (API fields it reads still exist; compiles). Nothing written into any `_win_2026-10-04` folder; counts read-only on `_win_2026-10-03`; scratch under the session scratchpad only.
- **Faithful details copied:** coincident walk over the ring with wrap-around, erases `v_next`, restarts; at 3 sides each edge < 0.01 adds 1 and the walk ends. Collinear pass: plane by `|det| > 1e-4` in order XY, XZ, YZ (summed from vertex 1, same product order); edges <= 1e-8 skipped; the loop stops at the first sign flip (`break`, so collinear vertices after a non-convex turn are not counted, as in the source); +1 only when sides minus collinear < 3. Scope is `BUILDINGSURFACE:DETAILED` and `FENESTRATIONSURFACE:DETAILED` for flag and delete; windows are deleted on their own, hosts untouched; partner rules unchanged.
- **Deviations (code vs plan text):** (1) When no plane has `|det| > 1e-4` (all slivers whose projected area is below 5e-5 m2, at UTM coordinates also float noise), EnergyPlus does not use the surface's own vertices: `A`/`B` keep the previous surface's arrays (`:15716-15746`, the "non-planar" branch only prints a Severe and does not reassign them). Following the code, the module keeps one persistent `X,Y,Z,A,B` array set per IDF, processed in EnergyPlus order: `SHADING:SITE:DETAILED`, `SHADING:BUILDING:DETAILED` (`GetDetShdSurfaceData` `:3531`), then `BUILDINGSURFACE:DETAILED` (`:4294`), then `FENESTRATIONSURFACE:DETAILED` (`:5358`), each in file order. Without this, London was 39 of 40 (stem `0feee47fa59aacb6` ours 11, EnergyPlus 10). File order and name-sorted order both give 40 of 40. Shading events are counted for the state and the gate but shading is never deleted. (2) Only `UpperLeftCorner` + `CounterClockWise` + `World` is implemented (all EU IDFs use it); the `:9388-9421` re-ordering for other rules is not. (3) The "quad that becomes collinear" test is a 3-vertex surface (500 m long, 4e-7 m high): a 4-vertex quad with 2 collinear vertices has zero area and falls into the stale-array branch of item 1.
- **Test status:** 15 passed (8 new A2 tests + 7 orientation tests). No EnergyPlus run, no job touched.

Wall 0005_K1`): the XY projection of a vertical wall is a line, its `det` is float noise of about 5e-4 at 3e12-sized products and exceeds 1e-4, so XY is chosen and two vertices read as collinear (event 1). EnergyPlus's source does the same arithmetic, but no Lyon or Bologna `eplusout.err` was read (London only), so it is unverified that EnergyPlus really flags these walls. #### T06q — director audit of part A2; FINDING 288; D-EU-142; amendment B (director, 2026-10-01 22:50 EDT)

- **Audit A2: PASS.** Tests 15 passed. London 40 of 40 stems, N 205 = 205, 0 extra. Director re-check against EnergyPlus on the other two districts, on the `_fix_2026-10-01` runs whose IDF on Speed is byte-identical to the local copy (the remaining finished runs used an IDF that was swapped later, so they are not comparable): **Lyon 180 of 180 stems equal** (14 flagged by EnergyPlus, N equal on each, 166 with N = 0 on both), **Bologna 53 of 53 equal** (0 flagged on both). The rule is now verified on 273 runs in three districts. 5J's extra Madrid and Bologna rows (thin triangles under 10 mm high) are not flagged by EnergyPlus; our list follows EnergyPlus, and 5J is told so.
- **FINDING 288 — EnergyPlus mis-reads some real walls at map-grid coordinates (Lyon, Bologna).** EnergyPlus picks the projection plane with `|Σ(x_n y_{n+1} − x_{n+1} y_n)| > 1e-4` (`SurfaceGeometry.cc:15712-15746`). At UTM coordinates the products are ~3e12, so for a vertical wall the XY sum is rounding noise of ~5e-4, which passes 1e-4. The wall is then tested as a line, its corners read as collinear, and EnergyPlus counts a degenerate surface and cuts the wall to 3 corners (`:15837-15858`). The wall keeps its area (computed before, `:9539`), but its outline for sun and shading becomes a triangle, and the run carries a Severe error. Example: Lyon `cee45cbc2718154c` `Wall 0003_K1`, 6.36 m2, XY sum 4.9e-4; after subtracting a 1 km-rounded origin the same sum is 5.7e-10, and the wall is tested in the right plane. Seen on `_win_2026-10-03`: Lyon 15 homes, Bologna 1 home (largest 6.36 and 4.27 m2). London (national grid, products ~1e11) and Madrid (largest flagged 0.027 m2 wall, 0.105 m2 window) show none. These walls must **not** be deleted.
- **D-EU-142 (director, under the owner's standing rulings "continue however you recommend by considering always highest accuracy" and "ok continue to the end"):** move the Lyon and Bologna models to a local origin (a pure shift, nothing physical changes), then remove only the true slivers. Madrid and London are not shifted, so their unchanged homes stay byte-identical (5J's Madrid runs keep their value). Output goes to a new tree `_win_2026-10-05`, so the T06p gate on `_win_2026-10-04` is not disturbed.

**Amendment B (replaces item 3's tree and adds the shift; items 1–2 as amended by A2):**
1. New module `openubem/idf/local_origin.py`, one public function `shift_to_local_origin(idf_text_or_obj, dx, dy) -> idf`. It subtracts `dx`, `dy` from the x and y of every vertex of every coordinate-bearing object present in the trees. First list every object class present in the four `_win_2026-10-04` trees (one `grep -ho "^[A-Za-z:]*," | sort | uniq -c` per tree) and report which ones carry x/y coordinates (at least `BUILDINGSURFACE:DETAILED`, `FENESTRATIONSURFACE:DETAILED`, `SHADING:SITE:DETAILED`, `SHADING:BUILDING:DETAILED`, `ZONE` origin x/y if not zero). If a class with coordinates is found that this list does not name, STOP and report it. z is never changed. Same writer settings as `11_fix_orientation.py`.
2. Offset per district, FR and IT only: `dx = floor(min x / 1000) × 1000`, `dy = floor(min y / 1000) × 1000` over every vertex of the district's `_win_2026-10-04` tree (one constant per district, so neighbours keep their relative positions). Write it to `EU-11/<D>_win_2026-10-05/origin_offset.json` (`dx`, `dy`, `crs` from the district source, `applies_to` = every IDF). T07 must add it back when it draws plans from these IDFs.
3. `scripts/eu21/12_drop_degenerate.py` gets `--source-tree _win_2026-10-04 --target-tree _win_2026-10-05 --shift FR,IT`. For each district it copies the tree, shifts every IDF (FR, IT), then runs the A2 detector and deletes flagged surfaces (A2 rules), rewriting only IDFs that change. It writes `md5_2026-10-05.txt`, `degenerate_2026-10-05.csv` (stem, n_deleted, total_area_m2, largest_area_m2, names, kinds, boundary types, partner_not_degenerate), `dwelling_counts_2026-10-05.csv` (copy of the 10-04 one), `changed_stems.txt` versus `_win_2026-10-03` with `reason` = any `+`-joined set of `flat_count`, `degenerate`, `origin_shift`, and `fleet.lst` = every stem in `changed_stems.txt` (FR and IT: every stem).
4. Run order: **part B1 now on ES, FR, GB** (their 10-04 trees are complete: ES 1,172, FR 522, GB 1,240 IDFs, md5 lists full). **Part B2 on IT** is a separate dispatch after the T06p item-5 executor finishes the IT 10-04 tree. Never write into a `_win_2026-10-04` or `_win_2026-10-03` folder.
5. Tests (add to `tests/test_eu_degenerate_surfaces_2026_10_01.py`): a 6 m2 vertical wall at Lyon UTM coordinates is flagged before the shift and not after; every surface area, every outward normal and every pair of partner normals is unchanged by the shift (to 1e-6 m2 / 1e-9); the shift of a window and its host is the same.

**Gates (per district, report each line with the measured value):**

- (d) Shift check (FR): total outdoor wall area, total window area and total floor area per IDF equal before and after, to 1e-6 m2; the T06o orientation gate (0.05 m probe, pairs opposite, floors down, tops up, window = wall) passes on the 10-05 tree.
- (e) ES and GB stems not in `changed_stems.txt` are byte-identical to `_win_2026-10-03` (md5, N of N).
- (f) `partner_not_degenerate` count per district reported.
